  <h2>Glossary: every keyword and concept, defined</h2>
  <p>One hundred and ninety terms covering the corpus vocabulary end to end: order-book mechanics and market design, the chart languages (Wyckoff, VSA, Elliott, Market Profile), the quantitative core (no-arbitrage, martingale pricing, volatility modeling), the econometrics spine (stationarity, cointegration, regimes, extreme values), portfolio and optimization mathematics, and the execution layer. Each definition names the source it was learnt from where one applies. Everything here is teaching material: definitions are never findings, and nothing in this glossary is a trading signal under the programme's standing rules.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Adverse selection:</b> The informed-trade share of counterparty risk; the spread component compensating for losses to better-informed flow (Bagehot, Glosten-Milgrom).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Aggressor:</b> The trader crossing the spread and taking liquidity, a market order's initiator.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Bid-ask bounce:</b> Artificial reversal when consecutive prints alternate between bid and ask; under Roll, first autocorrelation of price changes = −c²/ (2c²+σ_u²) with γ1 = −c².</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>BBO / the touch:</b> Best bid and offer. All short-run price formation concentrates here (CKS: deeper levels add little at short horizons).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Bulk volume classification (BVC):</b> ELO's fractional trade signing: split each bar's volume through the normal CDF of the standardized price change. Robust to splitting and same-price fills.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Cancellation:</b> Removal of a resting order; ~45% of book events in ITCH data. In the OFI ledger it is identical to a same-side market order of equal size.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Circuit breaker:</b> Mandated halt on unsustainable moves; single-stock breakers were mandated after the Flash Crash.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Cross-impact:</b> One asset's flow moving another's price. Contemporaneous cross-impact ~nil once integrated OFI is included; lagged helps briefly then decays (CCZ).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Dark pool:</b> Off-exchange venue with non-displayed quotes. Darks + internalizers ≈ 40% of US equity volume by 2012.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Depth:</b> Size available at the quotes; second of Harris's four liquidity dimensions. Impact scales as c/depth (λ̂ ≈ 0.98, CKS).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Effective vs realized spread:</b> Effective = what the taker paid vs mid; realized = what the maker kept after prices settled. The difference is the informed-flow tax (Stoll, Madhavan).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Efficient price:</b> The hidden random-walk component m_t of observed prices; the martingale conditional expectation of value given the full trade history (Hasbrouck).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Event time:</b> Indexing observations by trades or volume instead of clock time (Mandelbrot-Taylor, Clark lineage).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Flash crash:</b> May 6, 2010: sell program + thin book + quote-withdrawal feedback loop → near-zero prints, ~1,000-point swing, broken trades nullified.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Free option:</b> A resting limit order's exposure to being picked off after news; quoting both sides writes two of them (Copeland-Galai via O'Hara, Harris).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Gambler's ruin:</b> Garman 1976: a zero-spread dealer's inventory follows a random walk and hits a ruin barrier with probability 1. The spread is a survival margin.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Hidden / iceberg liquidity:</b> Non-displayed parts of the book (iceberg orders show a slice); a third to a half of touch volume in ITCH evidence (Cartea).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Immediacy:</b> The ability to trade now; a priced service (Demsetz), bought by market orders from limit orders.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Information share:</b> Hasbrouck: each market's share of the common efficient-price random-walk variance, who does price discovery (futures vs cash).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Integrated OFI:</b> CCZ: the l1-normalized first principal component of depth-scaled multi-level OFIs; explains 89% of level variation.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Internalization:</b> A dealer fills client orders internally instead of exposing them to the lit book; pairs with payment for order flow.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Kyle's λ:</b> Price impact per unit of net order flow in Kyle (1985); the inverse of depth, the informed trader's constraint. Ancestor of all impact coefficients.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Latent liquidity:</b> Resting demand/supply not yet visible in the book, drawn out by price moves (Bouchaud's framework).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Limit order:</b> An offer to supply liquidity at a specified price; earns the spread, writes a free option, lives in a queue.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Maker-taker:</b> Exchange pays liquidity makers, charges takers; invented at Island (~1999–2000). Ties venue revenue to order-flow volume, a structural conflict.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Market collapse:</b> Glosten-Milgrom: when adverse selection is severe, no spread supports a viable market, the lemons problem applied to securities.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Market order:</b> Instruction to trade now at best available; demands liquidity, pays the spread.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Microprice:</b> Bid/ask midpoint weighted by queue sizes, a better short-run fair-value anchor than mid (Cartea).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Noise trader:</b> Uninformed flow that pays spreads and provides the informed trader's camouflage (Kyle). 'Noise is the lubricant' (Madhavan).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Order anticipator:</b> Front-runner, sentiment reader, squeezer: parasitic on large traders (Harris). Adds volatility, adds no liquidity.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Order flow externality:</b> Liquidity attracts liquidity (Harris); Island's law; Pagano's tipping – three names for the same network effect.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Order splitting:</b> Slicing size across many orders; the source of buys-follow-buys autocorrelation in trade direction (Hasbrouck's reality check).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Pace / time-between-trades:</b> Easley-O'Hara (1992): trade arrivals follow a state-dependent Poisson; the arrival rate itself carries information.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Payment for order flow:</b> Brokers sell retail orders to internalizing dealers, uninformed flow is a paid-for resource, the venue's fuel.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>PIN:</b> Probability of informed trading, αµ/(αµ+2ε): the informed share of flow. The product αµ is identified even when components are not.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Pricing error:</b> s_t = p_t − m_t: the stationary, transitory component of observed prices (bounce + inventory effects).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Queue position:</b> Precedence in the FIFO book. An asset with fill-probability value; decays because most queued size cancels before executing.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Resiliency:</b> How fast the book heals after being hit; fourth liquidity dimension (Harris, O'Hara).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Roll model:</b> Half-spread estimated from price-change autocovariance alone: c = √(−γ1). 'The little black dress of microstructure models' (Hasbrouck).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Scratch trade (0+ scalping):</b> Guaranteed no-loss trade exploiting queue priority and order-type exceptions (Bodek/Dark Pools).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Sequential trade model:</b> Bayesian quoting trade-by-trade as the flow reveals type: Bagehot → Glosten-Milgrom → Easley-O'Hara lineage.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Short-term alpha:</b> Transient drift faced by a market maker from informed flow; controls quote-pulling in Cartea's market-making problem.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Square-root law:</b> ΔP ~ √VOL, the classic empirical impact finding. Per CKS Prop 1, an aggregation artifact with a random slope; Bouchaud reads it differently, dispute open.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Spring / shake-out:</b> Engineered markdown to panic weak holders; on light volume with a high close it removes supply (Wyckoff, VSA), bullish, not bearish.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Stop-run fade:</b> Dalton: spikes beyond balance extremes with low acceptance tend to revert to value, the auction's own mean-reversion rule.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Test (VSA):</b> Low-volume dip into prior high-volume ground closing near the high: supply has been removed. High-volume test = failed; expect a retest.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Tick:</b> Minimum price increment; the atom of market structure, sets the cost of queue precedence and the balance of spread vs flicker (Harris, decimalization).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Time precedence:</b> FIFO priority for same-priced orders after price priority; the queue's constitution.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Toxicity:</b> Expected adverse-selection intensity of incoming flow; VPIN is the best-known real-time estimator, contested, classification-dependent.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Trade imbalance (TI):</b> Signed volume via trade classification, e.g. Lee-Ready. R² 32% vs OFI's 65%, and collapses jointly, OFI absorbs it (CKS).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Up-thrust:</b> New high intrabar, close at the low, after a rise: a stop-hunt trap, classic weakness (Williams).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Value area:</b> Market Profile: the 70% of activity (~1 SD) around the point of control; trades outside it are rejection/excess (Dalton).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Value trader:</b> The ultimate liquidity supplier: steps in when price departs from value, absorbs dealer inventory, defines the outside spread, makes markets resilient (Harris).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Volume clock:</b> Sampling one observation per fixed traded quantity (ELO): de-heteroskedasticizes, weights events by information content, near-iid returns.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>VPIN:</b> Volume-synchronized PIN: bucket-imbalance toxicity measure. Use CDF positions, not levels; a patent-encumbered contested proxy.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Winner's curse:</b> Fills are adversely selected: your resting order executes exactly when the price was about to move away from you (Harris, Foucault pick-off risk).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Alternation (Elliott):</b> Guideline (~90% reliable): Wave 2 and Wave 4 alternate in depth (and classically in form, simple/deep vs complex/sideways). Copsey quantifies it as the (ii)+(iv) retracement-sum band of 80-120%.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>American option / Snell envelope:</b> The American option's value process is the Snell envelope: the smallest supermartingale dominating the payoff. Early exercise is optimal at the free boundary where smooth pasting holds (Elliott-Kopp).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Cause and effect:</b> Wyckoff law 2: effect proportional to cause. The accumulation/distribution campaign inside the range is the cause; the P&amp;F count measures it and projects the move out (Forte).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>CVaR / expected shortfall:</b> The average loss beyond the VaR quantile. Coherent (subadditive) where VaR is not, it cannot punish diversification (Elliott-Kopp ch 11 proofs).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Data snooping / Reality Check:</b> White (2000): the null is the FAMILY null, the best of all searched rules has no edge. The Bootstrap Reality Check re-samples to ask how often pure chance would crown a best this good.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Deflated Sharpe ratio (DSR):</b> Bailey &amp; López de Prado (2014): adjust the observed best Sharpe for the expected maximum of N trials. SR 2.5 at N=88 is REJECTED (DSR 0.9004); the same SR at N=46 passes.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Diagonal triangle:</b> Elliott wedge at terminations (5th or C-wave position): sub-waves usually threes, 1/4 overlap allowed, ~80% occur as the 5th of a 3rd of larger degree; the reversal retraces to its start (Balan).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Effort vs result:</b> Wyckoff law 3 and VSA's core instrument: volume is effort, price progress (spread) is result; divergence between them reveals which side dominates.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>False discovery rate (FDR):</b> Benjamini-Hochberg (1995): control the expected proportion of false discoveries rather than the family-wise error. Power 0.42 → 0.65 vs Bonferroni when all nulls are false; destroyed by editing the family after seeing results.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Fifth Measurement Method:</b> Balan's projection: Wave 5 peak = (Wave 3 peak − Point 0) x 0.618 + end of Wave 4.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>GARCH:</b> Conditional heteroskedasticity as an ARMA process in squared shocks: persistence α+β &lt; 1, fat tails from Gaussian inputs alone (Jensen), asymmetry only via variance-asymmetry (GJR/EGARCH) or a skewed law (Levy, Tsay).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Harmonic Elliott Wave (HEW):</b> Copsey's revision: every impulse (i)(iii)(v) is internally three waves (a)(b)(c); the market is a nested 3-wave fractal; wave (b) is the 5.6-100% wild card that replaces extensions, failed fifths and most diagonals.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Kelly criterion:</b> Bet a fraction of capital proportional to edge/odds. Overbetting converts a positive edge into certain ruin; every blowup in The Quants (LTCM, Citadel 2008) is a Kelly violation via leverage. Fractional Kelly for uncertain edges.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Long memory / Hurst exponent:</b> Persistence beyond short lags (R/S, ARFIMA). Spread series: H ≈ 0.33-0.41 vs ~0.5 for stocks, and pairs profitability ranks with (0.5 − H). Diebold-Inoue: rare-break regime switching mimics fractional integration.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Martingale measure (EMM):</b> A probability measure under which discounted prices are martingales. Exists iff no arbitrage (First Fundamental Theorem); unique iff complete (Second). Hasbrouck's efficient price is the same object under the true measure.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>One-timeframing:</b> Dalton: in an up-auction, no subsequent period takes out the prior period's low. Fading it is a costly error; it is the simplest encodable trend-day state.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>P&amp;F count:</b> Point-and-figure box count across the trading range: Wyckoff's parameterized measure of the cause, projecting the move out and setting the 3:1 reward/risk gate.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Probability of backtest overfitting (PBO):</b> Bailey et al (2017): the probability that the in-sample-optimal configuration underperforms the out-of-sample median, computed by CSCV (combinatorially symmetric cross-validation). Requires an honest trial registry.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Regime switching:</b> Hamilton (1989): an unobserved Markov chain drives the observable series; the Hamilton filter computes exact likelihood and filtered state probabilities. The N vs N+1 likelihood-ratio test is invalid (parameters unidentified under the null).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Retracement / projection ladder:</b> Copsey's ratio grids: retracements 9.02%…100%, projections 105.6%…594.4% of the prior wave; the discovered 223.6% ratio links strongest 3-wave legs. Dense enough that some ratio always sits near any reversal, matched nulls required.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Sinkhorn / optimal transport:</b> The minimal-cost mass-moving plan between two distributions. Entropic regularization (Sinkhorn iterations) makes it tractable; W_p with cost = distance^p is a true metric. Basis for book-state distance and WATCH change detection (Peyré &amp; Cuturi).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Structural break:</b> Bai-Perron: breaks estimated by global SSR minimization over trimmed partitions via dynamic programming; UDmax double-maximum first, then sequential l vs l+1; trim at least 0.10 with heterogeneity or serial correlation.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Surrogate data:</b> Theiler et al (1992): test a pre-registered statistic against constrained realizations of a null (phase-randomized, AAFT): M = 1/α − 1 surrogates for a rank test (19 for 95%). Test the null, never the result.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>The creek / the ice:</b> Wyckoff's wavy lines: the creek is resistance across rally peaks inside an accumulation range (the JAC crosses it); the ice is support under reaction lows in distribution (breaking through invites 'drowning').</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Transfer entropy:</b> Schreiber (2000): asymmetric measure of directed information flow, the driver's history reduces uncertainty about the target beyond the target's own history. The formal replacement for Granger-causality claims, for large samples only.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>TPO / POC:</b> Time-price opportunities: 30-minute letters stacking into Dalton's profile histogram. The POC is the price with the most time behind it, a gravitational reference; the value area is 70% of TPOs (~1 SD) built around it.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>UTAD:</b> Upthrust after distribution: late-range new highs on rising volume with relative narrowing spread, then return to the range's mean close, encodable distribution signature (Forte); distinct from the early-range minor UT.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>VaR (and why it is not coherent):</b> A loss quantile. Fails subadditivity: merging portfolios can raise VaR, punishing diversification (worked counterexamples, Elliott-Kopp). Estimate it, then Bernoulli-hit backtest it.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Volatility smile:</b> Systematic implied-vol variation across strikes, 1987's scar tissue. Its existence falsifies the constant-volatility model; the implied-vs-realized skew gap (29/30 periods) is the consistency problem.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>AVFI:</b> The programme's frozen volume-flow spread indicator: weekly z-score of the spread between a volatility-floored, log-normalized flow measure and its own slow moving average. State descriptor; no timing edge survived testing.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Rule set T:</b> 12-week momentum, long/flat, long 100% or 0%: the one survivor of the programme's family (full-span Sharpe 0.571, maxDD -23.5% vs -52%), failed holdout significance at p=0.069 exactly as the 43% power analysis predicted.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Holdout battery:</b> The sealed 2021-01-01..2026-11-13 weekly window (307 weeks), spent once on 11 Nov 2026 under pre-committed tests and a no-re-tuning clause. Nothing passed; it is never re-evaluated.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Kill line:</b> A pre-registered threshold that determines a hypothesis's death before the result is seen. Never moved after the fact, the programme's entire evidential value rests on that immutability.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Vacuous filter:</b> A rule that can never fire on real data (e.g., the |sp_z| &gt;= 1 transition filter: zero trades ever, because transitions occur where the spread is near zero). Test that a rule can trade before testing whether it wins.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Aha map:</b> The ten-stage causal chain (S0 two queues and a memory through S9 findings instantiated) that rebuilds the whole corpus with the because-it-must-be-otherwise-it-breaks method. Every keyword in this glossary is downstream of the S0-S5 spine.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Mechanism vs edge:</b> The map's biggest conceptual risk: real measured mechanism (spreads are insurance, impact is depth-scaled) never implies a tradable edge at any particular horizon. Discriminator: real at what horizon, in what units, net of what costs, against which controls, at what power.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Horizon law:</b> Cross-stage law L2: predictability is a property of the sampling grid. OFI's 65% R-squared lives at 10 seconds and decays within about two average price changes; a weekly bar averaging millions of events cannot inherit it. What survives compression is the slow state.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Salient Market Hypothesis (SMH):</b> The programme's endpoint: a first-of-market, falsifiable theory of liquidity and time, earned through the sealed prediction record rather than declared. Its standing axioms: the default assumption is no edge, and the burden of proof is absolute.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>ECS:</b> The programme's frozen evidence-claim scorecard, v0: kill lines at 3, 8, 19 and 31 events for 10, 20, 40 and 60 scored rows; the positive proof is exactly 57 of 104 resolved claims, not a single headline discovery.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Pre-registration:</b> Committing hypotheses, tests, thresholds, families and power in writing, dated, before any outcome is read. The entry ticket that makes a null count as evidence instead of a disappointment.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Amendment (programme sense):</b> A dated, signed change to a registered test. Withdrawn amendments stay withdrawn, and nothing is re-tuned after a null; an unsigned amendment blocks evaluation entirely.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Occupancy:</b> How many sample points a state or event class actually contains. Zero occupancy makes a hypothesis untestable regardless of plausibility (H7: the bear-capitulation state had zero weeks in all 307 holdout weeks).</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Circular-shift null:</b> A persistence-preserving alternative: rotate the predictor series against the outcome instead of resampling within it. Valid where within-sample bootstraps are not, i.e. near-integrated series with lag-1 autocorrelation near 1.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Parity mirror:</b> A second implementation of the same tests in another language (the programme's TypeScript mirror of the pandas chain). Substitutes for code review when no second coder exists; caught two real pre-first-run bugs in the forward-test tooling.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Sunday cadence:</b> The programme's weekly mechanical loop after the battery: refresh completed bars, resolve the oldest pending week at its close, append the decision entry. Guards refuse pre-close, stale-fetch, duplicate and out-of-order bookings.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Standing gates:</b> The owner-only decisions no delegated session may touch: arming a test, ratifying an amendment, moving a kill line, opening a sealed panel, and trading. Work generation was delegated; proof autonomy was refused by design.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Stage pipeline:</b> The pre-registered validation ladder: data audit, replication, event studies, base case, robustness, costs, reliability (family tests, power, one-shot holdout), forward paper trading, and governance. Each stage's verdict is append-only.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Matching engine:</b> The deterministic program that clears the limit order book by priority rules (price, then time or pro-rata). The Cont-Degond-Xuan axioms are its acceptance test: flow plus the clearing rule must reproduce the book's dynamics. (O'Hara; Harris; framework paper)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Pro-rata allocation:</b> A matching priority that fills resting orders in proportion to displayed size rather than arrival order. Encourages queue-padding with unfillable size; one reason displayed depth can overstate true depth. (Foucault-Pagano-Röell)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Liquidity sweep:</b> A marketable order that consumes multiple book levels at once. In VSA terms a climactic event (effort far beyond the usual, result large); in ledger terms, aggressive demand integrated across the touched levels. (Williams; Cont-Kukanov-Stoikov)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Market maker / dealer:</b> A trader who quotes both sides continuously and earns the spread while absorbing inventory and adverse-selection risk. The whole inventory branch of microstructure (Garman, Amihud-Mendelson, O'Hara ch.2) is the study of when this is survivable.</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Inventory model:</b> The spread family where quotes are set to manage stock risk rather than adverse selection: the dealer widens on excess inventory, mean-reverts it over time. Fingerprint: spreads correlate with inventory and time of day, not with trade direction. (Stoll; O'Hara)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Stealth trading:</b> Splitting an informed metaorder into small slices and opportunistic timing so footprint per trade stays small. Evidence: price impact concentrated in medium-sized trades. A falsifiable prediction about how informed flow must behave. (Madhavan survey)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Commonality:</b> Liquidity co-moves across securities (Chordia-Roll-Subrahmanyam; Hasbrouck-Seppi), implying a systematic liquidity factor. Microstructure risk is market risk: spreads and depth load on common factors, which is why one book's conditions cannot be treated as one book's idiosyncrasy. (Madhavan; Foucault-Pagano-Röell)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>SOES bandit:</b> The 1987-1997 Nasdaq exploit: small-order execution system fills at stale quotes after news, systematically picked off. The first machine-era lesson that stale quotes are a free option waiting to be exercised. (Harris; Patterson)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Open outcry:</b> The pre-electronic continuous auction in a physical pit. Its vocabulary (the tape, the pit, the crowd) survives in every tape-reading lineage from Wyckoff to VSA; its economics did not survive the matching engine. (Harris)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Accumulation range:</b> The Wyckoff process by which commercial interests transfer inventory from weak hands near the lows: preliminary support, selling climax, automatic rally, secondary test, then spring shake-out, cause building, sign of strength. Each named phase is a testable flow condition, not a squiggle. (Forte; Pruden; VSA)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Preliminary support (PS):</b> The first noticeable demand within a decline: volume expands, the close strengthens off the low, but the down move resumes. Encodable as a volume/range condition inside an established downtrend. (Forte; Pruden)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Selling climax (SC):</b> The panic peak of a decline: maximum urgency, wide-range bars, extreme volume, a strong close off the lows. The demand that meets the panic becomes the cause for the subsequent advance. Programmatically measured (H6's bear-capitulation state was derived from this idea). (Forte; VSA; Pruden)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Automatic rally (AR):</b> The mechanical rebound after a climax, leg-for-leg profit taking without smart-money demand. Its top defines the upper boundary of the developing range (the creek). (Forte)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Secondary test:</b> The re-approach of the climax low on visibly smaller volume and range: the confirmation that the transfer of inventory succeeded. Passes or fails as a data test (Pruden's Test 3), not by narrative. (Forte; Pruden)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Sign of strength / weakness (SOS / SOW):</b> The confirmation phase-leaving move: a wide-range advance on expanding volume leaving the range, or its bearish mirror after distribution. Distinguishes a campaign from a range that is merely rotating. (Pruden; Forte)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Backup (return to the creek):</b> The orderly retest of the range top on declining volume after an SOS: the classic Wyckoff retest and a direct ancestor of the buy-the-dip condition (H2 tested a version of it and failed; the idea is fine, the edge was not there on weekly bars). (Pruden; Forte)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Last point of support (LPS):</b> The higher, lower-volume pullback after a successful backup: demand absorbing supply at successively higher prices inside the markup. The point Wyckoff would call the last low-risk entry, and the reason its identification is a probability, not a date. (Pruden)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>No demand / no supply:</b> VSA's smallest informative bars: a rally attempt on below-average volume (no demand, bearish) or a dip on below-average volume (no supply, bullish). Professional absence as a signal: effort withdrawn when the move is unsupported. (Williams)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Stopping volume:</b> A high-volume bar at the extreme of a decline suddenly closing off the lows: real size entering and absorbing the panic. The single bar that distinguishes climax from continuation. (Williams)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Wave personality:</b> The expected character of each Elliott position: wave 2 sharp and deep, wave 3 strong and extended, wave 4 sideways, overlapping and dull. Partly quantifiable (range, overlap, relative position) and the basis for the count-verification lessons. (Balan; Copsey)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Guideline of equality:</b> Alternating waves of the same degree tend toward equality in price length when unequally time-consuming: an a priori target generator, never evidence of anything. The distinction targets are drawn from; the falsification is in the count, not the ladder. (Copsey; Balan)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Extension (Elliott):</b> An impulse wave that travels far beyond its siblings (usually the third). Post-hoc identifiable only in degree retrospect; a standing warning that target ladders are drawn after the fact, and the programme's reason for refusing to trade them un-counted. (Copsey)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Truncation (failed fifth):</b> The fifth wave that fails to exceed the third's extreme, typically after a violent opposite-sided event. Rare, and unknowable in real time; the honest label for why every real-time ending-count carries a truncation alternative. (Balan; Copsey)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Zigzag (Elliott):</b> The 5-3-5 corrective pattern: sharp, against the larger trend, deepest of the corrections. Its sharpness is the factual basis for alternation (expect flat, sideways fourths after zigzag seconds). (Balan)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Flat (Elliott):</b> The 3-3-5 sideways correction, classically a deep 90 percent retracement of wave 1. The pattern basis for the expectation that retests reach the prior wave top without invalidating the count. (Balan)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Contracting triangle:</b> The 3-3-3-3-3 correction of narrowing oscillation (the consolidation that precedes the final move of a larger sequence). Distinct from the diagonal triangle, which sits in a trend position and implies termination. Its measured move rule is a pure projection rule: drawn, never tested. (Balan; Copsey)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Cleanout:</b> Copsey's terminal pattern: a final low to complete wave 3 at the same time as the wave-5 low, requiring the next wave up to be immediate. The thesis justifying the trade-the-next-wave-not-the-channel reading of Lesson 12; un-provable on existing tests, which is why it is taught as structure, not signal. (Copsey)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Fractal claim:</b> The HEW assertion that every impulse is internally three-wave even as it externally shows five: the price of the cleaner ratios is a changed, non-RN-Elliott grammar. Teaching it as claimed, not verified, is the lesson. (Copsey)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Market profile:</b> Dalton's (via Steidlmayer's) bell-curve record of where price spent time: TPO counts per price, the day framed by an initial balance then range extension. Blocked in the programme's source copy (OCR-dead); taught here via CMT coverage and flagged as such. (Dalton, proxied via CMT III)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Auction balance / imbalance:</b> The profile's regime split: balance is rotation about a value area, evidenced by time building a visible TPO peak; imbalance is the one-timeframe move that relocates value. The ancestor of the regime-awareness taught in Lessons 12 and 22: do not trade rotation logic in a relocation phase. (CMT III, Dalton-flagged)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>No-arbitrage:</b> The founding constraint: no portfolio costing nothing can return a sure profit. In discrete time it is the separating hyperplane argument (Elliott-Kopp); in continuous time it produces the martingale measure (Björk). Everything in the quant core is some version of this single sentence. (Björk; Elliott-Kopp)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Fundamental theorem of asset pricing:</b> First theorem: absence of arbitrage implies the existence of an equivalent martingale measure. Second theorem: uniqueness (completeness) is rare, not the default. All pricing is expectation under the risk-neutral measure; all hedging difficulty is the second theorem failing. (Björk; Elliott-Kopp)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Complete market:</b> The exceptional case where every derivative is replicable by the underlying and the money market, making the price unique. Every smile, every model zoo in Javaheri, and every why-can't-I-hedge moment comes from markets being incomplete. (Björk; Elliott-Kopp)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Black-Scholes-Merton:</b> The 1973 formula and its assumptions (constant vol, continuous trading). Its role in this programme is falsificationist: the volatility smile exists because Black-Scholes is false, and every smile trade is a bet on which part of the falsification you own. (Björk, derived)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>The Greeks:</b> The sensitivity of a derivative's price to its parameters: delta (spot), gamma (delta-of-delta, curvature rent), vega (volatility), theta (time). The delta and gamma books of a market maker are literally inventory in vol-space, and the hedging flow of large gamma is a microstructure force. (Björk; Javaheri)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Implied volatility:</b> The vol parameter that sets a model's price equal to the market's: the market's own forecast, polluted by appetite for risk. The implied-vs-realized consistency problem (implied higher in 29 of 30 periods) is the honest finding that separates the two. (Javaheri)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Local volatility:</b> Dupire's approach: fit one surface so every vanilla option is priced at once. The fitted surface is an ill-posed inversion of noisy option prices: unstable, extrapolation-hostile, and a standing example of a big artifact sold as a big pattern. (Javaheri)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Stochastic volatility (Heston family):</b> Volatility itself modeled as a mean-reverting random process, correlated with the spot. The vol-of-vol parameter is nearly unestimable at daily frequency (Javaheri's identification hierarchy): a precise, correct warning that a model can be true and still unusable. (Javaheri; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Jump diffusion:</b> A price process with a continuous component plus a rare, instantaneous jump component. The mathematical acknowledgment that some days are not samples from the same generating process, the formal version of real-world events like gaps. (Javaheri; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Leverage effect:</b> The empirical asymmetry: negative returns raise future volatility more than positive returns of the same size. The reason GJR and EGARCH exist (Tsay) and the reason the programme measures drawdown asymmetry rather than total variance. (Tsay; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Volatility clustering:</b> The second universal stylized fact: large changes follow large changes, not uniformly but in bursts. GARCH is its measurement instrument, and volatility updating is what separates a live risk model from a dead one (Härdle's VaR stress tests). (Tsay; Härdle; Bouchaud)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Leptokurtosis (fat tails):</b> The first universal stylized fact: return distributions have tails heavier than the Gaussian, with sample kurtosis that refuses to die. Tsay's GARCH decomposition splits observed kurtosis into jump-driven bursts and model-driven mixing; using Gaussian tails for risk numbers is the original sin of VaR. (Tsay; Bouchaud; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Volatility signature:</b> The measured curve of realized volatility as a function of sampling frequency: flat across sensible frequencies, exploding at the finest scales from microstructure noise (the bounce). The formal tool for choosing the sampling grid, and the reason tick data measured naively is contaminated. (Bouchaud; Tsay)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Hawkes process:</b> Self-exciting point process: each event raises the intensity of further events. The probability model for clustering in event time (trades, quotes, extremes), and the statistical frame that makes event clustering a measurable parameter rather than a mood. (Tsay; Bouchaud)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Metaorder:</b> A single investor's order split into many pieces by an execution algorithm. The market never sees the metaorder, only the children it emits, and every order-flow signal is an inference problem about the hidden parent. The concept unifying stealth trading, volume-clock theory, and square-root impact. (Bouchaud; Cartea; Johnson)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Itô calculus:</b> The integration theory for processes with continuous but rough paths: the chain rule gains a second-order term. The entire continuous-time quant core and its dX-squared equals dV bookkeeping live here. (Björk)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Girsanov theorem:</b> The measure-change tool: shift probabilities to make a drift-away process a martingale without touching the paths. The mechanism of risk-neutral pricing (real drift becomes irrelevant under the new measure). Teaching it as the manipulation of beliefs, not of physics, is the honest frame. (Björk)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Numéraire / change of unit:</b> Pricing relative to a different asset (the money market, the forward, another currency): each choice of numéraire yields a different but equivalent martingale measure. The formal version of the lesson that a reference frame must be stated to make any ratio meaningful. (Björk)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Optimal stopping:</b> The mathematics of choosing the best time to act: value as a supremum over stopping rules, solved for American options by the Snell envelope and in trading by entry/exit bands (Cartea's pairs geometries). Also why a triggered rule (a trend labeled ex post) cannot become an ex-ante datepicker. (Björk; Elliott-Kopp; Cartea; Maheu-McCurdy)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>HJB equation:</b> The Hamilton-Jacobi-Bellman condition: the value function of a control problem satisfies a nonlinear PDE whose solution certifies optimality (the verification step every solution in Cartea goes through). The mathematics that turned execution from folklore into a solvable problem. (Björk; Cartea)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Merton problem / two-fund separation:</b> The optimal portfolio for an investor with power utility: a riskless fund plus the growth-optimal fund, with the split set by risk aversion. The first honest division between what an allocation actually depends on (one number, risk aversion) and what is sold to investors (a hundred weightings). (Björk)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Market price of risk:</b> The single number that converts a unit of volatility exposure into expected return premium: the market's per-volatility exchange rate, invariant across all assets once the numéraire is fixed. Everything with an excess return claim gets measured against it. (Björk)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>ARMA / ARIMA:</b> The linear filter bank of time-series econometrics: autoregression on past values plus a moving average of past shocks, ARIMA with differencing for trending levels. The most basic identification discipline: what the autocorrelation structure allows and what it does not. (Tsay)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Unit root / ADF test:</b> The diagnostic for distinguishing a stationary series from a random walk with drift: regress the change on the lagged level and test against the Dickey-Fuller distribution. The difference is not cosmetic: a unit-root series wanders, so its sample moments fail the law of large numbers. (Tsay)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Cointegration:</b> Two non-stationary series whose combination is stationary: they share a common trend and a stationary spread. Engle-Granger and Johansen trace tests. The statistical license for pairs trading, and for the correlation-when-integrated caution, the ancestor of the programme's near-integration warnings. (Tsay; Cartea)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Error-correction model:</b> The representation of cointegrated series: each series adjusts toward eliminating the disequilibrium in the spread, with adjustment coefficients that differ per series. A pairs bands strategy (Cartea ch.11) is an error-correction mechanism with an explicit stopping rule. (Tsay; Cartea)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Vector autoregression (VAR):</b> The multivariate workhorse: each variable regressed on lags of all variables, with impulse responses and variance decomposition to separate mechanism from echo. Hasbrouck's information share is a VAR variance decomposition; a spurious lead-lag claim is a VAR claim that did not survive its own machinery. (Tsay; Hasbrouck)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Kalman filter:</b> The recursion that estimates an unobserved state (the efficient price, time-varying betas) from noisy observations, optimally under Gaussian assumptions. The modern descendant of every estimate-the-latent-price problem the programme inherits from Hasbrouck. (Tsay)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>MCMC / Gibbs sampler:</b> Simulating a posterior distribution by sampling each parameter conditional on the others. The Bayesian estimation route for stochastic volatility (Javaheri ch.4), where the latent variance path is not an argument you can maximize directly. (Tsay; Javaheri)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>EWMA / RiskMetrics:</b> The exponentially-weighted volatility filter: a single decay parameter (0.94 at daily) instead of a full GARCH fit. The J.P. Morgan simplification that made vol updating industry-standard; its bias and its inability to capture asymmetric response are the price of the simplicity. (Tsay; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>GARCH asymmetry (GJR / EGARCH):</b> The asymmetric-response corrections: GJR adds a term when returns are negative, EGARCH models log-variance so positivity is automatic. They exist because the leverage effect is real and unmodeled symmetric GARCH is miscalibrated in exactly the states that matter, downside. (Tsay)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>TAR / STAR models:</b> Threshold and smooth-transition autoregressions: regimes as observable, data-determined switching rather than latent Markov states (the regime family's non-Hamilton branch), with delay and threshold estimated, not asserted. The two regime traditions in one frame: visible threshold vs latent probability. (Tsay)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>GEV distribution:</b> The generalized extreme-value limit law for block maxima: Fréchet (heavy), Gumbel (light), Weibull (bounded). The formal instrument for the worst-week question the bear-capitulation trades live on, and the reason a five-sigma Gaussian surprise is a category error on weekly data. (Tsay)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Peaks over threshold (POT / GPD):</b> The EVT alternative to block maxima: exceedances over a high threshold converge to the generalized Pareto family. The standard tool for drawdown-tail estimation and the formal basis of the programme's standing caution that weekly-finance tails are not Gaussian in the extreme region. (Tsay; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Hill estimator:</b> The direct tail-index estimator: fit power-law decay to the k largest observations. Its k-choice is arbitrary and its estimate drifts with k: the artifact-prone honest tool that pairs with the mean-excess plot's linearity check. (Tsay; Bouchaud)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Mean-excess plot:</b> The EVT diagnostic: for a GPD tail, the expected exceedance beyond u is linear in u; curvature rejects the fit. The visual form of the honesty rule: the tail index is a property of the chosen threshold, and reporting it without the check is junk statistics. (Tsay; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>ACD duration models:</b> Autoregressive conditional duration (Engle-Russell): the time between events clustered in the same way returns cluster. The direct descendant of the pace/time-between-trades signal at the heart of the volume-clock theory. (Tsay; Easley-O'Hara)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Near-integration:</b> A series with a root close to, but not exactly at, one: almost all finite samples are indistinguishable from a unit root, and its autocorrelations and Hurst-type statistics are distorted for the same reason. The technical home of the programme's D5-family invalidity warnings. (Tsay; Diebold-Inoue)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Filtering vs smoothing:</b> Filtered probabilities use data up to time t only (what is knowable in real time); smoothed probabilities use the full sample (what is knowable in hindsight). The ex-post dating critique is a filtering vs smoothing error: publishing smoothed regime labels as if they were filtered. (Tsay; Maheu-McCurdy)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Mean-variance / efficient frontier:</b> Markowitz selection: minimize variance for a target return, tracing a frontier whose interior is dominated. Honest companion facts: naive equal-weight often wins out of sample (DeMiguel et al.), and estimation error, not math, is the binding constraint. (Bartholomew-Biggs; Härdle)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>KKT conditions:</b> The Karush-Kuhn-Tucker stationarity-and-complementarity system that characterizes the solution of a constrained nonlinear program, inequality constraints included. The verification step for every construction method, and the honest reason a solver's answer without the certificate is just a suggestion. (Bartholomew-Biggs)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Trust region:</b> The optimization method that models the objective locally and trusts it only within a radius: robust to the difference between model and landscape, the default for the ill-conditioned, the nonconvex, the badly-scaled. (Bartholomew-Biggs)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Sequential quadratic programming (SQP):</b> The nonlinear constrained solver family: each iteration solves a quadratic approximation with linearized constraints. Fast and sharp near the solution, the engine behind most portfolio construction that respects realistic constraints. (Bartholomew-Biggs)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Interior-point / barrier method:</b> Solve the constrained program by adding a barrier that repels iterates from constraint boundaries, then relax the barrier. Handles large inequality systems elegantly; the standard modern solver architecture. (Bartholomew-Biggs)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Global optimization (multi-start / DIRECT):</b> Explicit defense against local minima: many starts, or an algorithm (DIRECT) that provably explores every shrinking box. The optimization twin of the programme's multi-start verification culture: a flat, deceptive landscape admits it only under systematic search. (Bartholomew-Biggs)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Downside risk:</b> The risk family that measures only the harmful side: semivariance, target semivariance. Decision made under its discipline are rank-equivalent to Markowitz under elliptical distributions but diverge when tails differ, which is exactly why the programme's drawdown-first bookkeeping, not variance-first, is the honest choice for weekly data. (Bartholomew-Biggs)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Tracking error:</b> The standard deviation of the active return versus a benchmark: the constraint of portfolio construction (tracking error budgets) and the honest unit for how far an allocation deviates. (Johnson; Cartea)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Implementation shortfall:</b> Perold's name for the true cost of execution: the gap between the paper portfolio decided at arrival and the real executions with their slippage, fees, timing and opportunity costs. The single number that keeps an ex-ante paper result from flattering its ex-post execution, the pivot of every honest TCA. (Johnson; Cartea)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Arrival price benchmark:</b> The mid at the moment the decision was made, the riskless-IS benchmark: the reference frame that makes any improvement from algo choice or delay an explicit, attributable number. TCA's torsor: change the reference frame and every number changes meaning, which is the Reid-Szendrői lesson in execution clothing. (Johnson; Cartea)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>TWAP / VWAP / POV:</b> The benchmark algorithm family: equal slices through time, slices proportional to expected volume, or a constant fraction of realized volume. Each is a point on the urgency dial; each is a bet on where volume will be, not just when. (Cartea; Johnson)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Urgency (the dial):</b> The single parameter that indexes the solution of any execution problem: trade more now (higher temporary market impact, less timing risk) or more slowly (less impact, more risk). Cartea's solution surfaces the trade-off explicitly, the practitioner's choice of algorithm is a rough position on the same dial. (Cartea; Johnson)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Participation cap:</b> A hard bound on the fraction of volume the algorithm will take: urgency with a ceiling, protecting against being the market in thin conditions. Programmes teach it as a constraint type (a deterministic safety bound), not a performance feature. (Johnson; Cartea)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Futures roll:</b> The scheduled exit from an expiring contract into a later one, with the calendar spread's noise included in any measured return across the roll date. Index-fund Sharpe ratios should be cross-checked on cash-equivalent instruments; roll dates are on the calendar, not in the tail. (Johnson; Biggs exercises)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Fair value / index arbitrage:</b> The known cost-of-carry relationship between an index and its futures: deviations are tradable within transaction costs, with no directional information left for an observer after the fact. The historical ancestor of the discipline that mechanical relationships produce spreads, not prophecies. (Johnson; Madhavan)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Event volatility (NFP / witching):</b> Scheduled calendar events with measurable impact signatures: employment releases carrying roughly 89 percent of a day's volatility per standard-deviation surprise one way, quarterly expirations mechanically distorting volume. Every event backtest needs its calendar-matched control, not just its regime-matched one. (Johnson)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>Sniping / layering:</b> The two speed games around a queue: taking stale quotes before their owners can cancel (sniping, the free option in action), or displaying size with no intent to trade to shape the book (layering, manipulative and illegal). Both are reminders that the book is an adversarial surface with its own incentive compatibility problems. (Harris; Bouchaud)</p>
    <p class="gl"><b>FIX protocol:</b> The industry text protocol for order routing: the grammar of the internal market. Studied at the vocabulary layer (message types, order types, execution reports); the infrastructure lesson that plumbing errors surface as trading errors downstream. (Johnson)</p>
  <h2>Method &amp; integrity</h2>
  <ul>
    <li>Canonical weekly bars: 6,646 daily NQ=F, 22 repairs logged, 22 roll/crisis divergence weeks vs NDX all explained.</li>
    <li>Costs 0.3625 pts/side (1 tick + commission). Bootstrap: stationary, B = 2000, block = 13, seed = 42.</li>
    <li>Every rule faces matched random-entry and buy-hold controls before it is believed.</li>
    <li>All development on 2000-2020 only; the 2021+ holdout was opened exactly once, on 2026-11-11, with the registered Amendment 2 battery; it is spent and will never be evaluated again.</li>
    <li>The SMH design layer is pre-2021 unsealed bookkeeping only: no forward statistics exist, the forward log is empty by design, and every claim's pass/fail schedule was pre-declared before the first forward row. Amendments 6 (forward evaluation protocol), 7 (judgment log) and 8 (clock study) are drafted and shelfed unsigned; Amendment 10 (volume/Hawkes) is drafted and unsigned, data-blocked; Amendment 9 (cross-market) was SIGNED 2026-11-14 and its Sunday macro booking runs on autopilot (first live reading booked no rows); Amendment 12 (crypto accelerator) was SIGNED 2026-11-14 and executed exactly once at its own gates: the C-W3 BTC arm closed untested at the occupancy gate (ledger v2 records the closure and the barrier-spec repair awaiting ratification), and the remaining open WHEN claim stands as the unsigned Amendment 13 candidate draft (citation-audited before signature); an options term-structure unseen-ness pre-registration stands as the unsigned Amendment 14 candidate (D-3, the readout frozen before any data decision); the accelerants inventory (D-5) is shipped as record upkeep; nothing in any unsigned amendment is evaluated.</li>
    <li>The crypto L2 branch is gated at pre-registration. Directives asserting non-existent scripts, gate runs, or a completed 17.8 GB download were refused at their own gates and each refusal is on the record; the honest Tardis finding stands (the U/u/pu sequence gate can never run on Tardis pre-normalized incremental_book_L2 files; the sequence-capable routes are the Bybit orderbook archive or a self-recorded raw depth stream, with Tardis kept as the engine cross-check). The branch waits on the local download per the published runbook; a dated amendment defines the real gate before any computation. The OHLCV-priced arms are not blocked on L2: C-W3 ran its gates and closed on occupancy alone, and the C-W1 candidate (weekly BTC OHLCV) is pre-registered and awaits only a signature.</li>
    <li>Both one-shot holdout batteries were dry-run validated on unsealed data before the real run: two latent crash bugs were caught and fixed first, and one dry-run window bug briefly touched the sealed span before quarantine; every incident, including that one, is recorded in the programme log.</li>
    <li>All analyses share one pandas reference implementation; the TradingView Pine is parity-checked against it line by line. The verification chain rebuilds the working tree from the durable bundle on every environment reset and runs 25 automated checks end to end, including the V4h booking-audit selftest (12 seeded bookkeeping violations must all be caught).</li>
    <li>Records are append-only. When concurrent sessions write from stale copies, the damage is repaired by dated restoration notes and verified re-runs; nothing is silently rewritten, and every repair is itself logged. Defective frozen numbers, when a pre-registered anchor exposes one, are corrected on the record with the defective value preserved - history stays auditable. A two-tree drift (durable bundle vs live working tree, the bundle stale since 12 Nov) was found and re-synced on 2026-11-16, diff-verified, with the cadence tools' hard-coded live-repo paths noted as the root cause.</li>
  </ul>

    the programme is restricted to paper trading under any outcome. Built for Andrea Karl Vassallo,
    University of Malta MD candidate, who does this in the evenings after medicine lets him go.

  <h2>What died, what survived</h2>
  <div class="card">
    <p class="bad">Died: every AVFI entry rule (27 variants, jointly and individually), the state-transition system, the bear-capitulation timer (one episode, extinct states since 2010), every parameter "plateau", and the tops/bottoms reversal flags (H8/H9: divergence fades wrong-signed, climaxes too rare to prove). The two follow-on hypotheses (H10/H11) were withdrawn before a single forward return was computed: their tests were either invalid as drafted or blind to anything realistic. Withdrawing an underpowered test is recorded as a win, not a loss.</p>
    <p class="ok">Survived: a one-line trend rule (12-week momentum), whose out-of-sample magnitude replicated almost exactly (0.571 unsealed, 0.575 on the holdout). The indicator's only real job is as a risk throttle on position size, not as an entry timer.</p>
    <p class="quote">The indicator was a slow regime descriptor all along. The edge, such as it is, is generic trend. The sophistication budget is best spent on sizing, not entries.</p>
  </div>

  <h2>The strategy: T vs U3, after the holdout</h2>
  <p>Rule set T is the tracked rule: long at next open when the 12-week log return is positive at the prior close, flat otherwise, signal exits only. U3 keeps the T signal and varies only sizing: vol targeting to 20% annualized plus a 0.5 throttle in AVFI reversal_down states.</p>
  <table>
    <tr><th>metric</th><th class="num">T, holdout 2021-2026</th><th class="num">U3, holdout 2021-2026</th></tr>
    <tr><td>annualized Sharpe</td><td class="num">0.575</td><td class="num">0.418</td></tr>
    <tr><td>bootstrap p (0.05 bar)</td><td class="num">0.069</td><td class="num">0.116</td></tr>
    <tr><td>total return</td><td class="num ok">+57.2%</td><td class="num">+26.4%</td></tr>
    <tr><td>max drawdown</td><td class="num">-23.4%</td><td class="num ok">-13.0%</td></tr>
    <tr><td>trades / exposure</td><td class="num">19 / 71%</td><td class="num">41% (avg size 0.58)</td></tr>
  </table>
  <p>Reading: neither rule clears the pre-committed significance bar. T's out-of-sample Sharpe matches its unsealed estimate to three hundredths; what it missed was the p-value, precisely the outcome the power analysis gave 57% odds. U3's in-sample Sharpe advantage vanished out of sample, while its drawdown control partially held (-13.0% vs -23.4%): crisis insurance underperforms when the regime is not 2008-like, which is the trade you make when you buy it. T is tracked forward; U3 is logged in parallel with no claimed support. The weekly decision card (signal, regime, vol factor, throttle, paper size) is also on TradingView as the AVFI v3.5 research panel: observation only.</p>

  <h2>Lessons learned</h2>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">01</span><h3>The indicator was the least important part</h3><p>Months of Pine craft, 27 variants, zero passes. A one-line trend rule beat all of them. Craft is not edge.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">02</span><h3>Controls or it did not happen</h3><p>The state system scored 0.42 in validation and looked fine until matched random entries scored 0.465 on the same window. Every edge is now judged against random-entry and buy-hold anchors.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">03</span><h3>One-episode edges are seductive</h3><p>Bear-capitulation: +0.23 sigma next week, p = 0.03, textbook. All 90 of its weeks were one 2008-2010 episode, and the state has been extinct since 2010. Significance from one regime is history, not edge.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">04</span><h3>Elegant conditions can be vacuous</h3><p>The pre-registered |sp_z|&gt;=1 filter produced zero trades ever, because transitions happen exactly when the spread is near zero. Test that a rule can trade before testing whether it wins.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">05</span><h3>Costs were not the enemy</h3><p>Tripling slippage moved Sharpe by under 0.012. Absence of edge was the enemy. Cost paranoia is misplaced at this holding period.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">06</span><h3>Parameter sensitivity usually hides a slowness artifact</h3><p>The H5 "plateau" decomposed into pure slow-EMA drift. When a parameter works only at extreme values, find the simple thing it proxies.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">07</span><h3>Data discipline precedes strategy discipline</h3><p>Repairs logged one by one, cross-checks against the cash index, single-source risk quantified before any rule ran. Bad data produces confident nonsense.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">08</span><h3>Reproducibility is insurance</h3><p>Eleven environment wipes, eleven identical rebuilds, same seed, same numbers. A result that cannot survive a rebuild was never a result.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">09</span><h3>Power analysis before the irreversible decision</h3><p>The holdout opens once. The pre-computed odds said a true 0.57 edge passes only 43% of the time. The battery then returned p 0.069 against a 0.05 bar: the predicted miss happened. Knowing the odds first is the difference between testing and gambling; accepting the verdict afterward is the difference between research and rationalization.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">10</span><h3>Entries got the attention; sizing was the untouched lever</h3><p>The first sizing change in the whole programme improved drawdown by a third in-sample. Out of sample its Sharpe edge vanished but its drawdown control partially held. Sizing changes what you lose, not what the market owes you.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">11</span><h3>Magnitude can replicate while significance fails</h3><p>T's holdout Sharpe 0.575 vs unsealed 0.571: as honest a replication as this craft gets. It still returned "not supported", because 19 trades over 6 years cannot clear a 0.05 bar reliably. A consistent edge and a statistically proven edge are different claims; confusing them is how research programmes go broke.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">12</span><h3>Insurance is only worth its premium in the insured regime</h3><p>U3 throttled size through a holdout that held one short bear and one long bull. The premium (a third of the upside) was paid every week; the payout never came. Regime-conditioned sizing must be judged against the regimes that actually arrived.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">13</span><h3>Near-integrated series break within-sample resampling</h3><p>The spread's z remembers itself at 0.985 weekly. The drafted H10a test false-alarmed at 7-16% against a 5% bar, because one realization of a near-integrated series cannot reproduce the across-realization variance of its own statistics. Persistence-preserving nulls or event studies only; power readouts before signing; withdrawal is always allowed.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">14</span><h3>Validate your implementation against someone else's</h3><p>Our exact dynamic-programming break scan agreed with the ruptures reference only after it exposed a real off-by-one in our first recurrence; the pandas-Pine parity table caught the stdev convention bug the same way. Independent parity checks are how a solo research programme substitutes for code review.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">15</span><h3>Sigma-relative is not sigma-level</h3><p>The clock's compression event is a 2.5-sigma week against a quiet base - a small absolute move. Equity drawdown mass is made of scale and lives in stress. Sizing the armed windows cut drawdown by exactly zero while giving up a third of the return: the clock times scale-free events, the drawdown is made of scale. Name which one a rule targets before wiring it to exposure.</p></div>
  <div class="lesson"><span class="n">16</span><h3>Pre-registered anchors audit history, not just pipelines</h3><p>The sp_z study's first act was to reproduce a frozen prior number - and it could not, because the session-32 LOYO pair had been frozen from scratch prose with no instrument behind it. The correction is on the record and frozen into the selftest. Anchor-validation clauses are cheap; without them, defective numbers survive forever by being quotable.</p></div>

  <h2>The verdict is in: stage 7 begins</h2>
  <div class="card">
    <p>The holdout was opened once, on Andrea's explicit go, with the registered Amendment 2 battery. On 307 sealed weeks (2021-01 to 2026-11): H7 untestable, the momentum_down state went extinct; T Sharpe 0.575, p 0.069, no formal pass; U3 Sharpe 0.418, p 0.116; family max-t p 0.09 and 0.14. Nothing passes.</p>
    <p>The honest reading: the trend prior is real-looking and consistent, its statistical proof is beyond what 19 trades can deliver, and no registered weekly-timing claim survives at the pre-committed standard. Per the registered null-outcome clause, the holdout is spent and forward paper testing is now the only evaluation path. The stage 7 paper log runs T as the tracked rule and U3 in parallel, every week logged including flat weeks, 26 entries minimum before any evaluation, kill criteria pre-committed. First regular Sunday session: 2026-11-15, after the 2026-11-13 close.</p>
    <p>The Sunday loop is fully automated end to end: a data refresh, an outcome resolver that books each closed week at the Monday open with the logged fill convention, and a position-aware entry generator that drafts the next entry from the data. Guards refuse pre-close booking, duplicate resolution, out-of-order weeks, and stale data. Two real booking-tool bugs were caught in rehearsal before the first live run; the completed-week guard was hardened so a partial Friday bar can never be booked as a decision close.</p>
  </div>

  <h2>The second hunt: the Salient Market Hypothesis</h2>
  <div class="card">
    <p>With every entry-timing claim dead or withdrawn, the research question changed: stop asking <em>when to enter</em> and ask <em>when the market's own volatility clock makes a significant week more likely</em>. The result is the Salient Market Hypothesis (SMH), built as the measured replacement for Andrea's hand-drawn Elliott Wave counts: a three-region vol-regime clock (compression / mid / stress) on weekly NQ, with a survival-analysis engine underneath it.</p>
    <p><strong>The measured core (all design bookkeeping, pre-2021 unsealed data only).</strong> After a compression entry (n = 41), the Kaplan-Meier hazard of the first significant week (&ge; 2.5&sigma; move) reaches 0.634 within 12 weeks, against a climatology of 0.441, with disjoint confidence bands. The gradient is monotone and edge-local: compression 0.634 &gt; mid 0.470 (~ indistinguishable from climatology) &gt; stress 0.273. The doctrine that falls out: <em>the drama buys the stress; the stress decays into the boredom; the boredom buys the drama.</em> The aftermath is a circle, not a line: the first print is usually the only print (22 of 26 windows without a further one; the print inflates the very sigma yardstick that defines the next one), the machine returns to compression at a median 15 weeks, and the post-print clock sits <em>below</em> climatology. The event anatomy: the spell often dies of its own event; significant weeks carry median 1.14x baseline volume; the anticipated change is concretely a ~3.5&sigma; week overshooting its own bar by a median 1.41x.</p>
    <p><strong>What it is not.</strong> It is not private information: VXN at the compression entries sits at the 25.8th percentile (level-cheap) but the variance risk premium <em>survives</em> the trigger (+3.71 vs +4.55 vol points): the crowd's calm is overpriced calm even at the cross; unseen is the wrong word for cheap. One conditioning cell hints harder than the rest (residual fear at the cross, 0.800 vs 0.476) and is flagged, era-confounded, and never trusted until forward-scored. The covariate program is closed: the region is the whole signal at this grain.</p>
    <p><strong>The proof, pre-declared against an empty log.</strong> Nothing on the 2000-2020 sample can prove the hypothesis; the proof object is the forward calibration record, logged at clock entries before outcomes resolve. Every pass/fail schedule was declared before the first forward row exists: occurrence kill lines 3/8/19/31 at 10/20/40/60 scored rows (rows, never years; the trigger rate is regime-dependent at 0.7-3.2/yr while the capture fraction holds at 0.647/0.625 across halves), each gate's false-kill measured at ~3-4% (a true machine dies in public roughly one run in eight: the stated price of falsifiability), calibration expectations frozen (0.6568 vs 0.7319 nats against matched climatology rows), two direction peeks only, a seductive-cell register closed at three frozen cells, and the positive proof reduced to exactly one count: at least 57 of 104 resolved compression windows resolving with the event. Proof is slow by measurement: the first public gate is ~5 years out at the design rate. The forward log is empty by design and every schedule stands ready against it.</p>
    <p><strong>The definition audit: how much belongs to the phenomenon, how much to the definition.</strong> Both forward-scored claims were elasticity-tested over pre-committed grids, one knob at a time, every cell reported, none selected, the frozen definitions unchanged by fiat. The compression claim (11 cells: window 6/10/12 under both cut conventions, bar 2.0/3.0, cut re-quantiled around its true 27.52nd percentile - the "tercile" label is historical): the entry-minus-climatology gap is positive in every cell (0.106-0.222 around the frozen 0.193), the frozen definition is not the grid optimum (the anti-tuning evidence, carried as a standing selftest anchor), and the disjoint-band strength is definition-local (4 of 11 cells) - the "bands do not overlap" sentence is never quoted without the frozen definition attached. The stress de-stress claim (9 cells: a crossing has no bar knob, so the window and the cut only, rival the coin): the edge is positive in every cell (0.115-0.283 around the frozen 0.136), the frozen cell sits conservative in the lower third of the grid, and the band-clears-coin strength is again definition-local (5 of 9, not the frozen one - consistent with the stress gates' honestly weaker power). Structure seen: the bar scales both clocks together while the gap stays steady, and the crossing's cum12 is far less definition-sensitive than the event clocks - the level belongs to the definition, the gap (and the edge) is the phenomenon.</p>
    <p><strong>The five-layer architecture, mapped and closed (session 53-54).</strong> Andrea's strategy architecture was mapped document-by-document onto the measured state: market state and event hazard are built, frozen and selftested; the AVFI-precursor layer is measured and fully closed (transitions orthogonal to the clock, the corrected Cox says the hazard content is duration - the pooled level, not even its slope - and the sp_z cell is the last one closed); direction is deliberately forward-only; microstructure is data-blocked on the broker/CME export and the options term structure; exposure is closed by the sizing-study verdict (the registered vol targeting is the measured lever, the clock is not). The map exists so nobody re-asks what is already answered; what advances the architecture now is the Sunday cadence, the forward rows, and the export.</p>
    <p><strong>The corrected Cox number, and how it was caught.</strong> The pre-registered anchor validation of the sp_z study failed to reproduce the session-32 LOYO Brier pair (0.1596 vs 0.1574, a claimed 1.4% covariate gain) - the pair existed only in prose with no instrument behind it and is arithmetically inconsistent with the frozen coefficients. Corrected on the record, append-only: intercept-only 0.07364, duration-only 0.07406, full four-covariate 0.07607 (the covariates are 2.71% worse out of fold). The frozen verdict survives and strengthens; the defective number's fate is itself frozen into the selftest. Pre-registered anchors do not only validate pipelines - they audit history.</p>
    <p><strong>The null-time methodology review (v0, 16 Nov 2026, unsigned).</strong> With the entry-timing hunt closed and the forward rows accruing at the clock's own pace, the machine itself was audited during the L2 setup window. The verdict on the core: keep it. Pre-registration, fast-kill slow-proof economics, parity discipline, the multiplicity spine and holdout integrity are the reasons anything here can be believed. The measured cost is proof time: at the frozen ~2.04 clock entries per year, the first kill-line gate is ~5 years out, the first calibration readout ~13, the two direction peeks ~20 and ~29, and the 57-of-104 positive proof ~51 years on NQ alone. The signed cross-market clocks are the one standing accelerant (up to ~4x if the macro trigger rates compare, unmeasured until rows accrue), and the crypto WHEN claims add streams under the same family discipline when their data unblocks. The ceiling is the weekly grain itself: 0.634 at the cross versus 0.500 after four quiet weeks and 0.348 after six - the compression cross is the measured closest approach to the event, and finer timing needs intraday data the programme does not hold. Five draft candidates await signature (an accrual-rate readout line in the gates status, a family registry doc, an options term-structure unseen-ness pre-registration as a future amendment, an era-weighted reporting convention, an accelerants inventory page); an explicit not-proposed list holds the line against re-tuning, direction rules, holdout revisits, new weekly indicators and backtests-as-evidence. The binding constraints are data unlocks and forward time, not literature.</p>
    <p><strong>The null-time follow-on (17 Nov 2026).</strong> The review's first consequence arrived the same window: the signed crypto amendment's first arm ran its own staged admission gates on BTC daily and closed at the occupancy gate (7 primed entries against the required 10, untested, with a barrier-spec repair recorded for ratification before any forward row), which is the pre-registration discipline working, not failing. The family registry shipped as record upkeep (every registered claim in one table with family, multiplicity control and status), and the last open WHEN claim on BTC OHLCV was pre-registered as an unsigned Amendment 13 candidate: the NQ compression hazard transposed by rule, gated by the same occupancy test that just closed its sibling, direction refused, kill lines frozen from its own design window before any forward row, and zero status until a dated signature. The same day's follow-on: the C-W1 draft was audited constant-by-constant before signature (every quoted number verified against its source, all pass, one wording defect corrected on the record), the D-3 options unseen-ness question was pre-registered as the unsigned Amendment 14 candidate (the readout and its null frozen before any data decision, the data source his signature choice, the power ceiling stated plainly), and the accelerants inventory shipped as D-5 record upkeep (the only time-to-proof levers with their honest sizes, every seductive shortcut refused on sight with its citation). The unsigned draft candidates D-1 and D-6 remain decisions, not work; D-3 now stands as the Amendment 14 candidate awaiting the same word.</p>
    <p><strong>The 18 Nov window (sessions 90-94).</strong> The macro booking instrument rehearsed end to end, all green, and its Sunday task verified armed by direct connector read, ahead of the first fire window (Sunday 2026-11-22 23:45 Malta); the week's own live read booked nothing because the DAX compression reading is a dwell, not a cross, and the forward log's absence is by design. The owner asked directly whether the programme is close to an edge and the answer is on the record: no - one measured, definition-audited, three-market-replicated conditional hazard as design bookkeeping, with the four honest gaps (forward proof, direction, priced-ness, cost) named and scheduled. Two pasted third-party VPVI artifacts were provenance-checked, registered as unregistered-lineage reference material and never run, and the indicator's core was reviewed in verified mathematics: its "delta" is volume-weighted candle anatomy with the close absent from the formula, so reversal bars are misread by construction. The third book batch was dispositioned (22 duplicates, one genuinely new book queued). And the owner's demand for the framework-once was answered with the unifying clue dossier: every measured clue, ordered by closeness to the anticipated event, price then volume then time, nulls carried openly as exclusions, direction refused, kill lines stated. The queue of decisions that compresses the calendar remains his.</p>
    <p><strong>The study window finished (sessions 95-97).</strong> The site itself caught up through session 94, and the study programme then closed its open books: the third batch's genuinely new sources all studied and noted (the Wyckoff pair, Markets in Profile, CMT Level III, Patel &amp; Modi), and its one new book, Nakahara, read end to end with the honest verdict stated on the record and everywhere the book is represented: low direct relevance, methodological only, no market claims, five discipline-level lessons. The book library's skeleton edition now numbers 27. A follow-up pass citation-audited the study note's page pointers against the book's own OCR text, zero defects. Nothing registered was touched, nothing evaluated, nothing booked; the decision queue and the Sunday windows stand as shipped.</p>
    <p><strong>The verification window (sessions 99-106, 18 Nov evening).</strong> Between the Friday close and the Sunday cadences the honest work ran out, and the programme said so instead of manufacturing progress: every session re-ran the ground truth, the chain rebuilt from the durable bundle and green, and the one true statement it found each time is that no lawful work exists beyond the owner's signatures, his hands, and forward time. One genuine scare tested the discipline: the knowledge mount read empty in a reset environment and the turn correctly wrote nothing, a strictly read-only investigation; the mount returned intact and the rule it set is now permanent: a possibly-intact record is never rebuilt over. The owner's two BTC weekly captures (his wave counts, the AVFI panes, drawn lanes toward 2030) were registered as visual observations only: the beauty of a resolved chart is hindsight, and BTC's own measured clock does not exist yet - the C-W1 hazard awaits the unsigned Amendment 13 signature before its Stage 0x/A can compute BTC's entry count and gap under the occupancy gate that already closed the daily arm. The decision queue remains the binding constraint; of everything standing, only the L2 download and the V4h task-system check decay with delay.</p>
  <p><strong>The autonomy layer (session 111, 19 Nov).</strong> The owner accepted the two-turn autonomy proposal with "Go for it", and the programme shipped governing documents rather than a mood: the autonomy charter grants unattended sessions a standing authorization in eight lawful work classes while refusing, permanently and by design, autonomy over proof - no level may sign, evaluate, spend a sealed window, re-tune a frozen constant, or decide what counts as a result. The work list is now a self-maintaining queue with next-session directives, a staleness rule that ends the redundant verification streaks, and a rotation across session classes so the same task cannot monopolize the machine; every ten charter sessions, or on any clock entry, booking, gate event or closed verdict, a strategic reassessment must argue whether the decomposition still serves the objective, and any redirect sits at zero status until the owner's dated word. The mechanism is honest about what it is: prompt constancy plus state evolution, with novelty entering through data arrival, gate openings and forward rows - never through self-reinvention. The charter carries its own kill criteria, and no new scheduled task was created: installing the meta-prompt is the owner's instruction to give.</p>
  <p><strong>The loop deepens, and the platform front opens (sessions 115-119, 19 Nov).</strong> The class-U pass that carried this site to session 114 ended with its own correction, logged by append rather than rewritten: the closing tree re-sync was claimed before the copy ran, the between-turn reset had wiped the tree, the check itself caught the premature claim and the addendum states the rule the sessions now follow - expect the tree absent at any turn boundary, rebuild through the chain, never by hand. Class T then ran twice more and honestly: the seductive-cell promotion rule critiqued on the rule's own arithmetic (verdict and proposal R-1 in the table above, zero status until the owner's word), and the lexicon shipped with every frozen term traced to its source.
